### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

STOCK DATA

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Document 329: As good as it gets for the dollar_ 3 calls for FX

### Page 1

The dollar should stay supported by strong US growth, high
inﬂation and a Fed threatening to tighten before weakening
next spring as inﬂation falls. An AI correction looms large, and
what about attractive overseas growth drawing funds away
from the dollar?
Chris Turner
Better news from the AI sector is softening a key threat to the dollar
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING’s base call: Dollar stays supported
until next spring

### Page 2

Our base call assumes that the Federal Reserve stays
hawkish in the ﬁrst quarter, as decent US growth and above-
3% inﬂation keep money markets pricing in the threat of an
extended tightening cycle. That turns in the second quarter,
when energy-driven base eﬀects drop out of headline
inﬂation and a return to the Fed’s 2% inﬂation target sees
short-dated US rates move meaningfully lower.
That timing also suits events in Europe. French budgetary
politics look set to keep a risk premium in the euro ahead of

April's elections. We therefore see EUR/USD trading oﬀered in
a 1.10-15 range over the next four to ﬁve months. From the
second quarter, however, softer US rates, moderating energy
prices and clarity on French politics could trigger a gentle
recovery in EUR/USD towards 1.18 by year-end 2027.
Our risky call: AI bust
Energy and AI have been the dominant global macro drivers this
year and could well shape FX markets again in 2027. An AI bust is
not the base case, but if it occurred, it would lead to a lower dollar.

The IMF estimates the dollar could fall 6% under such a scenario.
The nature of the dollar decline could be erratic, however. If the
functioning of the ﬁnancial system were to come into question,
such as dollar liquidity drying up again, the dollar could ﬁrst rally.
But the inevitable Fed support measures to address liquidity needs
and the likely large rate cuts would ultimately push the dollar a lot
lower.
Our bold call: Overseas growth surprises

positively
EUR/USD rallied sharply in 2017 when Dutch and French elections
saw euro-sceptic parties failing to advance. Having toned down her
views on France leaving the euro, a decisive victory by Marine Le Pen
in April and greater budget clarity could reassure markets. If
eurozone growth continues to defy energy and political headwinds,

We’re also watching Japan for signs that it could follow South

Korea’s lead and deliver stronger growth. There, chip exports have
beneﬁted the broader economy and the Bank of Korea revised up

of Japan policy and a much weaker USD/JPY.

Document 348: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.
Exhibit 1: FDI Inflows to the U.S.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

from the upcoming electoral cycle

from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can

rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in

fiscal instability.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. Data releases confirmed resilient to strong growth and
above-forecast inflation in most advanced economies, with France lag-
ging behind and earning a rating outlook downgrade from Fitch as a
result. Fed, ECB and BoJ remained on hold, as expected, and on track
for December hikes. Oil continues to fluctuate between escalation fears

and hopes for a return to the negotiating table. The tech sell-off in
equity markets intensified despite strong earnings, as markets balk at
large capex plans. This helped bond yields ease from recent highs.
EUROZONE
Resilience confirmed despite tightening credit. GDP expanded by a
stronger-than-expected 0.4% q/q in Q3 (0.2% ex-Ireland), with Spain,
Germany and the Netherlands contributing to the bulk of non-Irish
growth. We increase our growth forecast to 1.6% in 2027 (+0.1pp),

driven by further improving sentiment in manufacturing. Bank lending
conditions tightened further in Q3 for businesses, especially SMEs,
driven by France and Germany, while corporate and housing-loan de -
mand weakened. The ECB kept its deposit rate at 2.5% and signaled it
was firmly on track for a (likely final) December hike. President Lagarde
also confirmed she would step down early, on February 7, setting off
the succession race. EU leaders are expected to decide jointly on the

three Board members departing between January and May (President,
chief economist, and market operations), likely at their mid-December
meeting. Official candidates to succeed Lagarde are BIS Head Pablo
Hernandez de Cos and former Dutch central bank Governor Klaas Knot.
To watch next week: October flash CPI (Wednesday).
France: Another growth setback, with silver linings
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 as a sharp drop in aeronauti -
cal production hit exports and investment. However, services out -

put grew by a robust 0.3% q/q, and household consumption also
grew more strongly than forecasted (+0.4%). While 2026 will ine -
vitably be knocked down (to 0.3%), we forecast 0.8% for 2027.
Nevertheless, Fitch issued an unscheduled rating outlook downgrade
to negative territory, on grounds that fiscal, growth and ad -
justment prospects have deteriorated since its August review.
A new review could be concluded as early as February. Harmonised
inflation accelerated to 3.8% y/y in October, with both energy and

non-energy contributing. Meanwhile, producer price inflation accele -
rated to 8.2% y/y in September. Insee’s index of household confidence
fell to 80, matching its 2013 all-time low, driven by a decline in ex -
pectations of its own financial situation. Private payroll employment
declined by 26,000 over Q3 (-0.1%). However, in previous quarters, the
decline in payroll employment was more than offset by the increase in
self-employment (not released until November 27).

To watch next week: September industrial production (Thursday); Sep-
tember foreign trade (Friday).
Germany: Growth holds up as inflation slows
GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3, with consumption rising slightly but in -
vestment declining and export growth slowing. HICP inflation fell from
3.3% to 2.8% in October following energy-tax cuts and fuel discounts,
but core inflation also slowed, to 2.1%. Business confidence (Ifo survey)
reached its highest level in more than three years, led by manufactu -

ring, which is back in positive territory for the first time since 2023.
By contrast, consumer confidence deteriorated to its lowest since May,
due to growing concerns about inflation, which are weighing on consu-
mers’ spending intentions.
To watch next week: September retail sales (Monday); September fac-
tory orders (Thursday); September industrial production (Friday).
Italy: Stagnant growth meets energy-driven inflation surge
GDP was flat in Q3, missing expectations, as positive domestic demand

was offset by net exports. Economic sentiment improved, but HICP in -
flation jumped from 4.2% to 5.6% in October, its highest level since
September 2023, mainly because of regulated energy prices; core in -
flation also rose, to a more moderate 2%. More positively, economic
sentiment rose to 100.2 in October 2026 (from 99.4 in September),
despite the Eurozone decline. The improvement was driven by services
and construction. The Italian cabinet approved a budget focused on

middle-class tax cuts, financed partly by levies on banks, energy and
gambling companies. x
UNITED KINGDOM
A cautious budget buys breathing space. The budget offered 98 small
cost-of-living alleviation measures and £4.1 billion in household ener-
gy cost relief, while only modestly increasing taxes, including a tem -
porary rise in the bank surcharge. Its short-term stance is slightly ex -
pansionary, while major structural decisions on defence, pensions and

welfare have been postponed. The official budget watchdog, OBR, cut
its 2027 growth forecast to 1%, and firms’ inflation expectations conti-
nued to rise. UK gilts outperformed and equities advanced after the
budget. GBP rose to a six-week high against the dollar and a 19-month
high versus the euro as markets price a near-certain Bank of England
rate hike (to 4%) at this week meeting. We agree but, unlike market
pricing, we do not expect it to hike further.

To watch next week: Bank of England interest-rate decision (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Fed awaiting more information despite strong growth. The Federal Re-
serve held its target range at 3.75–4.0%, with two members voting for a
25 bp increase, as Chair Warsh highlighted stronger growth, favourable
employment conditions and inflation that remains too high. Q3 GDP
accelerated to 3.4% annualised, powered by resilient consumption and
a 12.7% jump in business investment. Despite jobless claims remaining

near multi-decade lows, compensation costs adjusted for inflation de-
clined 0.4%, marking the second consecutive quarter in which labour
cost growth trailed price increases.
To watch next week: Midterm elections (Tuesday); October ISM ma -
nufacturing (Monday) and non-manufacturing (Wednesday); Q3 non-
farm productivity (Thursday); October non-farm payrolls (Friday).
JAPAN
BoJ on hold with underlying inflation “consistent with the 2% tar -
get”. The Bank of Japan kept its policy rate at 1.25%, with one dissent

in favour of a hike. Its outlook judged underlying inflation to be
consistent with the 2% target, a major shift in its assessment, although
Governor Ueda mentioned risks on both sides in his remarks, stres -
sing in particular the risk of inflation overshooting the target. It signals
continued gradual monetary adjustment, with a next hike expected in
December. Alongside the rate hold, the BOJ announced it would near -
ly triple the pace of ETF sales starting in January, to approximately

JPY 860 billion per year. Japanese equities shrugged off the announ -
cement.
To watch next week: Minutes of the September Bank of Japan meeting
(Thursday); September wage growth (Friday).

EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
China: Monetary policy reform and no growth signals
The PBOC disclosed a 1.25% overnight reverse-repo rate and an -
nounced that the overnight rate would become its principal policy
lever, starting 2nd November. It also pledged to develop cross-border
renminbi payments and introduced a liquidity backstop for non-bank

financial institutions. The 5th Plenum of the 20 th Central Committee of
the CCP focused on party discipline and crackdown on corrupt cadres,
without broader policy signals. Chinese equities had their worst month
since 2016, falling 8.5% in October, as doubts over AI earnings deepe -
ned investor pessimism. The PMIs published on 30 th October suggest
economic activity is losing momentum; in the manufacturing sector,
the RatingDog PMI fell slightly to 52 (from 52.1) while the official PMI

fell more sharply and dropped below 50 (49.3 vs. 50.1 in the previous
month).
South Korea and Taiwan: AI boom lifts growth even higher
In Korea, GDP grew by 1.0% q/q and 3.4% y/y in Q3, supported by boo -
ming semiconductor exports linked to global AI demand. The expansion
was uneven, however, as private-consumption growth slowed marke -
dly. In Taiwan, GDP surged by 3.2% q/q and 14.4% y/y in Q3, beating
expectations and accelerating from the previous quarter as technology

firms expanded capacity to meet strong demand.
EMEA
Poland: Lower inflation gives monetary policy some room
Headline inflation declined from 4.0% to 3.5% in October as electricity,
gas and transport fuel prices rose more slowly, helped by a temporary
fuel-price cap. Rates are likely to remain unchanged in the near term,
although renewed zloty weakness means a hike cannot be ruled out.
Saudi Arabia: Reserves fall as regional pressures intensify
Official reserve assets declined by 4.4% m/m in September to

SAR 1.75 tn, their lowest level since December, with foreign-currency
assets recording particularly sharp monthly and annual declines.
Türkiye: Pressure on lira and FX reserves
The lira headed for its largest monthly fall in 15 months and cen -
tral-bank reserves came under renewed pressure. This is the conse -
quence of the warning by MSCI that Türkiye could lose its emer -
ging-market status if measures to improve transparency and market
supervision following the Fund scandal are deemed insufficient.

LATIN AMERICA
Brazil’s post-election rout: stocks and real plunge, yields surge. Bra -
zilian markets, expecting a Flavio Bolsonaro victory, sold off sharply
after Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva won a fourth presidential term. The Ibo-
vespa fell 14% Monday — its largest daily drop since COVID-19 — and
ended the week down 18%. The real fell over 11% intraday and ended
the week down 8% against the euro. Meanwhile the yield on Brazil’s
10-year government bonds rose 250 bp to 15.5%, reaching its highest

level in over a decade. The selloff reflects fiscal concerns over pledges
to raise the minimum wage and Bolsa familia benefits and to offer free
public transport on weekends and bank holidays. Finance Minister Da-
rio Durigan’s pledge to seek a higher 2027 primary surplus, without a
new target, did little to reassure markets.
COMMODITIES
The oil market continues to fluctuate between escalation fears and
hopes for a return to the negotiating table. Brent prices were down by

5% over the week, reaching USD 109/b on Monday. Tensions on physical
supply continue to decline. Ditto for diesel crack spreads. On the back
of geopolitical tensions, lower-than-requested EU stock level and spe-
culation over a cold snap in Europe in November, European gas prices
(TTF) recorded another strong weekly rise and have breached the
EUR 100/MWh for the first time since Dec 2022). The Asian gas bench-
mark (JKM) is following the same trend but at a slower pace (4.6% w/w

to USD 32 /MBtu, equivalent to around EUR 96.3 /MWh).
TO WATCH NEXT WEEK
Global: Manufacturing PMIs for EM and final DM prints (Monday); final
services and composite PMIs (Wednesday).
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

### Page 1

Muted iPhone 18 Pro Cycle and Rising Memory Costs

[Table_Rank]

Maintained  Hold  
 Target price US$334

[Table_Summary] Lower TP to $334, Hold rating: Driven by investors being away from AI themes, Apple share 
price outperformed Nasdaq by 7.2% in the past one month. However, we continue to expect 
muted cycle of iPhone 18 P/PM with constrained volume of iPhone Duo. Additionally, memory 
cost hike will last longer than expected, driven by our view of tight DRAM/NAND supply, and 
we expect cost hikes throughout CY2027. For hardware, we still expect the iPhone 20 (2H27) 
to be a strong product cycle, thanks to new design of display, followed by Apple’s early move 
to TSMC ’s A14 in 2H28 (suggesting only 2 -years at 2nm). Additionally, Apple Intelligence 
remains the key to watch. Yet, the share price trades at 35x FY27E P/E, suggesting the stock 
isn’t cheap. By factoring in results, guidance and lower shipments/margins, we revise FY27E 
EPS by -10% and revise TP from $369 to $334, still based on 35x FY27E. 
Dec-Q margin guidance below expect ations: Apple delivered solid Sep t-Q results, with 
revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of $2.1 (+14% YoY), both ahead of 
consensus. For Dec-Q, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY (vs. consensus 
of +6.5%), Services growth of 8 –9%, and gross margin of 45 –46%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive to Street expectations, though the margin guidance and Services deceleration 
were disappointing, reflecting cost hikes and mix headwinds.  
The iPhone product cycle mixed, with challenges in FY2027: Mgmt sees a strong start of 
iPhone Duo but remains supply constrained. Hence, we continue to expect the EMS build plan 
of 6.5m of iPhone Duo by end-2026 with limited upside, while the mainstream iPhone 18 P/PM 
to be 74.5m. As said in our earlier report of iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max Lukewarm on Sept 15, we 
believe the muted demand will weigh on CY1H27 EMS builds. Margin wise, despite p ricing 
actions taken earlier, margin will likely be under pressure, as the CEO Ternus characterized 
current memory pricing as “unprecedented,” with higher costs in December and beyond.  
Apple positioning the rebuilt Siri AI as a core differentiator: The CEO Ternus emphasized 
that the new Siri is deeply integrated and privacy -preserving and is fielding ~2.5bn requests 
per day. From a financial perspective, CFO Parekh noted that these Si ri AI costs are 
embedded in the December margin outlook, suggesting near-term gross-margin compression 
alongside the memory cost up-cycle.  
Risks: 1) weak demand, 2) competition, 3) product delays, 4) geo-tension.

### Page 2

Earnings Forecast  
Solid September Quarter Results  
Apple delivered a solid September-quarter, with revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of 
$2.10 (+14% YoY), both ahead of consensus, reaching a record fiscal year of $479bn in revenue. Gross 
margin came in at 47.9%, aided by approximately one percentage point of tariff refund benefits (and a 
$0.05 EPS tailwind).  
iPhone revenue reached a September-quarter record of $57.6bn (+17.5% YoY) likely driven by higher 
ASPs, Mac rose a strong 24% to $10.8bn likely on edge-AI, Services set at $31.6bn (+9.8% YoY) which 
was below expectations, while iPad declined 10% to $6.2bn and Wearables fell 8% to $8.3bn. The 
company returned $28bn to shareholders. 
December Quarter Guidance Slightly Ahead in Revenue but Shy in Margin  
For the December quarter, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY ( vs. consensus of 
+6.5%), Services growth of 8–9%, and gross margin of 45–46%. Operating expenses are expected at 
$20.4–20.8bn, OI&E around $300 million, and the tax rate near 17%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive relative to prior Street expectations, though the margin range and Services deceleration 
reflect known cost and mix headwinds.

### Page 5

Valuation and Recommendation  
Hold with TP of $334 
We rate Apple (AAPL US) Hold and set our TP at $334 which is based on 35x our FY27E EPS estimate. 
We believe it is important to capture the 2H26 -3Q27’s iPhone 18 product cycle, given high market 
interest. We adopt P/E methodology as we believe the market now mainly focuses on the company’s 
earnings outlook, and we usually apply P/E method to downstream firms.  
We believe Apple’s ecosystem will remain strong and associated revenue durability is high. This is 
backed by Apple's installed base growth, double digit YoY growth in services. However, the market 
would wait for visibility for its Apple Intelligence. Meanwhile, the market will need more visibility on the 
higher-high retail prices for the iPhone 18 series due to the rising cost over tariff and 2nm/memories. 
That said, we expect its earnings growth to be  high-teens to 20% YoY in FY26E-FY28E, from single 
digit YoY in FY22-FY24. Considering >30x one-year P/E, the stock doesn’t look particularly cheap.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

### Page 1

1
國防能見度高，航空風險升溫
分析師／張乘維
研究範疇／工業 / 科技
工業產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/28
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《中東衝突升溫，地緣風險升國防優於航空運輸》
受到中東葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地多座機場 ，及烏
克蘭無人機攻擊俄羅斯能源設施導致布蘭特原油價
格10月再度攀升、帶動航空燃油價格同步上漲 ，高
油價再次衝擊航空運輸公司獲利。達美航空3Q26財
報，預告4Q26單位可用座位里程收入仍將增加，但
受到美國4Q26航空客運需求將下滑，以及航空燃油
價格上漲。達美航空也下調2026年全年獲利目標，
顯示地緣風險升溫再度衝擊航空運輸產業。
至於航空與國防產業受益於中東衝突與俄烏戰爭持
續消耗防空攔截器 、戰術飛彈及相關彈藥 ，帶動美

國及盟國由短期補庫存逐步轉向多年期採購 。全球
國防支出增加 、飛彈與防空系統補庫存 ，以及商用
航空引擎與維修需求持續成長 ，也讓產業巨頭雷神
(簡稱RTX)、奇異航天 (簡稱GE)、洛克希德馬丁 (簡
稱 LMT) 、 諾 斯 洛 普( 簡稱 NOC) 等 公 司 積 壓 訂 單
(Backlog)於3Q26均創歷史新高。

重點摘要
• 國防需求已由短期補庫存轉
為多年期擴產
《國防需求已由短期補庫存轉為多年期擴產》
美國主要航空與國防類股公布3Q26財報，中東衝突
再升溫導致國防需求高於商業航空 。以下是主要廠
商的分析 :
雷神∶受益於飛彈回補庫存 ，3Q26取得超過810億
美元商用與國防新訂單 ，其中國防訂單 590億美元 ，
包括約 230億美元 「戰斧 」飛彈訂單與 140億美元
「先進中程空對空飛彈 」（AMRAAM）訂單 ，帶
動總積壓訂單較上季增加 520億美元 ，累計訂單
3,400億美元 ，年增36%。在飛彈防空系統與商業
航空維修服務市場強勁帶動下 ，公司上調2026年營
收到973億美元、EPS 7.70美元(年增22%)。

洛克希德馬丁 ∶受益於飛彈與火控系統產量提升 ，
帶動3Q26營收與獲利優於預期。在PAC-3多年期合
約則推升下，積壓訂單增加616億美元達2,920億美
元。由於美國 、中東、北約國家飛彈補庫需求將持
續數年，所以公司上調2026年營收到810億美元(年
增8%)、EPS 30.55美元(年增42%)。
奇異航空 ∶受益於 LEAP交付 、備件 、維修及 shop
visit增加帶動 ，商用發動機及服務營收年增 21%，
3Q26營收優於預期。在商用航空備件、維修服務及
引擎交付需求優於預期 ，加上主要供應商材料投入
改善 ， 公司上調 2026 年營收到 508億美元 、EPS
7.75美元(年增26%)。
總結，雖然國防類股近期財報受益於產能提升維幅
上調全年財測 ，反觀油價高漲對於美國航空運輸業
造成全面性的衝擊，所以工業維持中立評等。

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《地緣風險衝擊中東信用基本面 ，但緩衝能力仍佳 》
中東戰事持續外擴 ，除了美伊相互襲擊使得荷姆茲
海峽情勢依然緊張外 ，葉門胡塞武裝也對沙烏地阿
拉伯密集發動攻勢 ，柴油出口因此受阻 ，能源價格
處高位不墜 ，布蘭特原油 10月以來便維持在 100美
元/桶之上。過往中東國家往往是高油價的受惠者，
但受到能源生產和運輸中斷衝擊 ，國際貨幣基金10
月下調2026年中東及中亞地區經濟成長率預估值 1
個百分點至衰退，沙烏地阿拉伯更預估將衰退 3.3%。

資產 4Q26 殖利率目標價 投資展望

新興主要貨幣企業債指數 6.64% A~BB級企業相對穩健
新興投資級債指數 5.55% 亞洲企業債供給有限支撐買氣，中東良好債信吸引力仍佳
新興非投資級債指數 7.50% BB級具升等潛力，拉美非投資級債可望因油價上漲而受惠
新興當地貨幣債指數 6.80% 風險溢酬反應貨幣政策較差情境，升息預期淡化後殖利率下行
人民幣債指數 2.20% 景氣不均衡復甦，寬鬆貨幣政策為主要支撐力
亞洲當地貨幣債指數 4.60% 亞洲國家貨幣政策觀望，短線維持中性看法

重點摘要 沙烏地阿拉伯 (以下簡稱沙國 )雖極力避免直接捲入
戰爭，但仍遭受衝突外溢效應波及 。戰爭不僅導致
沙國大幅增加補貼 ，軍事和安全支出以及融資費用
上升均加大財政支出 ，因此，即便油價上漲抵消產
量下降的影響而使石油收入高於預期，沙國2Q26預
算赤字佔 GDP比重仍達 7.1%，遠高於年度目標的
3.3%。儘管近期公布的 2027年預算計畫 (PBS)目標
是將預算赤字佔 GDP比率從2026年的4.9%(沙國官
方最新預估 )縮小到2027年的3.6%，但若戰爭長時
間 延 續， 此 目 標 或 難 以 達 成， 目 前 估 計2027 年
17420億里亞爾的債務目標亦將輕易被超越 。不過
即使如此 ，沙國目前負債占 GDP比重僅約 35%，

2027年市場最悲觀預期也大約40%左右，仍是低於
全球新興國家均值約80%的水準。
中東另一主要發債國卡達對天然氣出口依賴度高 ，
天然氣出口受阻也使得國際機構普遍預估卡達 2026
年經濟衰退10%以上、幾乎是2010年以來最重，所
幸卡達2026年以前累積巨額經常帳盈餘及外匯儲備
作為緩衝，即使在今年3月外部環境面臨極大壓力下，
卡達仍有 199億里亞爾的經常帳盈餘 ，累積過去四
季經常帳占 GDP比重仍達 13.5%，現今外匯存底足
以彌補3月的貿易逆差，並可維持４年以上。這些數
據都顯示戰爭對中東國家的經濟及對外償債能力確
實已帶來短線壓力 ，但過去累積的龐大緩衝使其具
備良好因應能力 ，信用違約交換 (CDS)目前並未因
定價油氣供應長期中斷而大幅攀升 ，今年雖持續發
債但債務壓力並不高 ，IMF更預期2027年中東國家
經濟將成長6.3%。然中東國家對能源出口依賴度仍
高，能源出口是否長期受阻則是後續觀察重點。
重點摘要
• 沙烏地阿拉伯負債占 GDP比
重約 35%低於全球新興國家

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《經濟強勁帶來的高利率無損新興債利差穩步收斂 》
財政收支並非驅動新興主權信用利差的唯一因素 ，
總體經濟環境 、市場流動性均牽動利差走勢 ，因此
雖然自7月以來沙烏地阿拉伯等中東國家信用利差又
再擴大，但也大概在3年均值附近並不算高。以歷史
經驗來看，BB級主權國家利差通常對財政惡化程度
反應較大 ，可能是因為該評等僅低於投資等級一級 ，
財政變化容易牽動投資人對升等至投資級的預期與
投資部位的調整 ，而在投資等級的主權國家中 ，則
以匈牙利和波蘭這些債務負擔較重的主權債利差較
易受財政問題影響。
油價和利率是近期2項重要觀察變數，也牽動利差走
勢，不過由於新興主權債指數中約5成的組成為大宗
商品出口國、3成為石油出口國，反而能受惠油價上

漲，油價上漲時與利差走勢敏感度也較油價下跌時
低；然而 ，更重要的是高油價對通膨和政策應對的
二階影響 ，將導致高利率長時間延續的可能性並最
終衝擊實體經濟 ，進一步限制石油出口國利差收窄 。
以長期趨勢來看 ，利率水準的絕對值高低不必然影
響新興債利差 ，利率走勢反而和信用利差呈現小幅
負相關，利率走升反映的若是強勁經濟成長而非能
源供給衝擊 ，此時往往能伴隨信用利差收窄 ，反而
需留意的是利率波動放大及利率的大幅重新定價 ，
這才是牽動利差波動加大的主因 。目前總體經濟環
境部分或可與2004~2007年相比擬，當時在強勁成
長的背景下利率長時間在4%~5.3%高位震盪，但新
興主要貨幣主權債利差仍可收斂至 134點(此波低點
189點)，若經濟成長能在現今高油價和高利率環境
下持續強勁，利差則可望保持穩定。
重點摘要
• BB級主權債及債務負擔沈重
的投資級主權債利差較容易
受財政問題干擾
• 新興主權債指數中約 5成為石
油出口國 ，降低油價上漲對
利差走勢的敏感性
3
即使油價上升，新興債利差與油價也少見正相關

重點摘要 《景氣及企業獲利續成長 ，企業債信改善趨勢延續 》
三大信評機構持續認可新興市場四大區域企業信用
評等的改善，3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等規模大幅
提高至760億美元，其中獲上調規模達到910億美元、
創下2025年第四季以來的最高水平 ，也已是連續第
5個季度改善。
3Q26新興市場4大區域評等調升數量均高於下調 ，
其中以亞洲改善動能最為顯著 ，主要貢獻來自於亞
洲金融業 ，全年升評 /降評比率已來到 1.8倍，逆轉
2019年降評多於升評的劣勢；拉美企業仍是新興市
場中違約率最高的區域 ，降評債券數量與其他區域
比也確實較高 ，但降評企業家數已是過去 10年來最
低，加上阿根廷主權債信獲上調帶動企業債 (主要是
國有企業)信評調升，拉美航空業者如南美航空集團

(LATAM Airlines)在油價維持高位環境下仍於3Q26
獲信評機構調升至 BB+，在企業債信分化環境下仍
有亮點 ，因此第三季拉美企業淨評級上調規模達
370億美元，反而創下2025年第三季以來的最佳季
度表現 ，全年共有 234家拉美企業信評得調升 。新
歐中東地區債信今年算是少數債信惡化的區域 ，很
大部分是受到戰爭及高油價影響所致 ，不過奈及利
亞和南非等主權評級上調仍帶來正向效果 ，梯瓦製
藥(Teva)評等更是上調至投資等級，帶來了120億美
元的貢獻，部分抵消地緣政治風險帶來的影響。
強勁的獲利成長持續支持新興企業穩健基本面 ，有
限的違約率輔以韌性十足的總經環境 、以及活躍但
不過度集中的初級市場 ，均是支持債信改善的原因 ，
預期在企業獲利仍看好的情境下 ，第四季企業債信
仍將朝正向發展。
• 3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等
規模大幅提高至760億美元，
創2025年第四季以來的最高
水平
• 新興市場4個主要區域評等調
升數量均高於下調 ，亞洲改
善動能最為顯著

重點摘要 《聯準會升息年 ，新興債雖有波動但正報酬機率高 》
觀察過往聯準會升息年度 ，利率溫和走升機率相對
較高，新興債市雖然多會受到升息壓力而出現一波
跌勢，但全年度仍有較大機率收紅 ，1996年以來僅
有2018、2022這兩個升息年度新興債市全年收黑 。
不過2022年新興債市重挫很大一部分原因來自於聯
準會在短短一年時間內將基準利率自0％升至4.25%，
這種猛爆性升息與目前所預設的風險管理式的溫和
升息環境有所不同；另外 ，2018年聯準會雖只升息
四碼，但土耳其及阿根廷因美元稀缺引發流動性風
險，這2國經常帳赤字占 GDP比重當時均高於 5%，
對外融資壓力上升的同時進而爆發金融危機 。然而
現階段新興國家經常帳已普遍改善 ，土耳其經常帳

赤字已降至 2%~3%，阿根廷更轉為順差 ，基本面
與2018年有很大的差異。若扣除這兩段期間，新興
債市在聯準會升息期間波動雖可能放大 ，但在貨幣
政策方向更趨明朗後 ，走勢便會止穩反彈 ，全年平
均報酬率仍有10%以上。
至於新興市場企業債部分 ，過去因升息所造成的跌
勢約是主權債的一半 ，主要是因為企業債發行天期
多較主權要來得短 ，加上該期間獲利動能強勁 ，因
此在利率上升時期表現往往相對主權債更具韌性 。
儘管利率走勢仍存不確定性，但最終若能穩定在5%
左右，預估新興企業債全年總報酬仍可維持在3%，
除非未來出現更大的外部衝擊 ，如利率持續大幅上
升引發對經濟成長受損的擔憂 ，或AI驅動的成長模
式被重新評價 ，否則對新興債市的看法仍將維持正
向，新興主要貨幣主權債在這樣的大環境下 ，短線
雖相對承壓，但後續反彈力道也可望大於企業債。
• 新興主要貨幣主權債在聯準
會升息年度正報酬機率近8成
• 新興企業債相對抗跌 ，公債
利率若穩定在5%左右，全年
總報酬仍可維持在3%
5
資料來源： BofA ICE Indices、Bloomberg，統計1996~2026/10/9，中國信託商業銀行，

《消費量能未見失速訊號》
• 9月卡片消費YoY回升至+6.2，
顯示量能尚未失速
1
資產 4Q26 看法 投資展望
核心消費 中立 本行維持產業上下半年營收成長48：52的溫和成長預期，消費產業在旺
季前呈現「量能可望持平、獲利品質分化」，同時零售商備貨行為與終
端消費需求仍支持到貨量的基本盤，但消費者實際支出意願可能受到油
價／利率上升抑制，戰術性減碼受油價與信心承壓較重的旅遊休閒類股
4Q26預計呈「量能可望維持、獲利偏弱」格局
資料來源：美國銀行2026/10/15，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。

• 消費量能未見失速訊號 P.1
• 「K型」分化持續收斂 P.2
• 4Q26傳統銷售旺季Bottom-Up P.3
• 核心消費三個次產業需求驅動各異 P.4
• 成本與定價動能 P.5
消費產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/29
BofA信用卡數據9月卡片消費YoY回升、零售銷售續增(惟
剔除汽油站僅+0.2%),延續8月自7月疲弱中的反彈,為本行
對量販/民生通路保持加碼提供量能基準。
總卡片消費YoY成長率於6-8月連續降溫(6月6.3%→7月
5.0%→8月4.5%),剔除油品後同步走緩
(5.6%→4.3%→3.7%)，主因6月世界盃與線上促銷時序錯
位帶來的高基期效應消退。9月YoY回升至6.2%、剔除油品
為5.1%(BofA指出皆為近四年第三強),惟季調後MoM僅
+0.2%(7月-0.2%、8月+0.9%),剔除油品後幾乎持平,顯示
YoY反彈部分來自油價推升,月度動能趨於溫和。投資意涵：
量能未見明顯失速，不支持對消費產業全面削減曝險,宜改
採結構性次產業輪動

《「K型」分化持續收斂，中低所得消費有收入基礎》
低所得家戶的消費與稅後薪資成長自 7月起皆居三分位
（Tercile）組之首 ，高所得 （不含前5%）已被中 、低所
得超越。三分位組中，低所得家戶的消費與薪資成長皆最
強；前5%仍是消費成長最快的族群 ，但已較高點放緩 。
各組消費成長均高於稅後薪資成長約1個百分點（pp）。

9月各所得階層信用消費成長率 vs. 稅後薪資成長率
中低所得消費有收入基礎 ，但低所得薪資成長由 5.2%降
至4.5%；前5%消費成長持續高於薪資成長 ，依賴財富效
果。

《4Q26傳統銷售旺季Bottom-Up需求強弱度分析》
NRF已於10月15日發布2026年假期旺季(11-12月)專屬銷
售預測：零售銷售預估年增4.2%至1.013兆美元(2025年
為+3.1%、9,720億美元；不含汽車經銷商、加油站與餐
飲)，成長率高於去年,但NRF亦提醒,通膨、油價上升與家
庭支出壓力對不同所得族群的影響將有差異。此前3月18
日發布的2026全年預測(+4.4%至5.6兆美元)則早於中東
衝突推高油價與聯準會重啟升息。

《核心消費三個次產業的需求驅動並不相同》

重點摘要
零售客流量表
5
民生必需品：P&G有機成長 +4.0%中，銷量貢獻 +2.4%，
價格貢獻有限；引用的 NielsenIQ資料也顯示家庭與個人
護理類 +4.3%、包裝食品 −0.9%。成長主要來自銷量與
組合而非漲價，且品類間分化明顯，這是本行僅給予中性
評等的主因。
餐飲：2Q26同店銷售呈現明顯分化 ，本行預估客流領先
的連鎖與咖啡系統維持正成長 ，倚賴折價促銷的速食與披
薩鏈許多為負成長 。在外食價格比居家食品高出約 1.7個
百分點的環境下 ，客流與價格的取捨將是 4Q26餐飲評等
的主要變數。
《成本與定價動能》
9月CPI顯示，整體CPI月增 +0.6%、年增 +3.7%，核心
CPI年增 +2.6%；能源年增 +20.4%、汽油年增 +34.2%，

是整體通膨上行的主因。對核心消費而言，關鍵在於食品
與能源的分離：居家食品月增 −0.1%、年增 +1.8%，外
食年增 +3.5%。
對毛利的含意：能源成本上升而零售端食品價格溫和 ，代
表核心消費業者較難以轉嫁成本 ，壓力更可能先反映在毛
利率而非銷售額 。P&G在指引中揭露本財年約 14億美元
稅後成本逆風（含其他逆風合計約每股0.79美元，約拖累
核心EPS成長11%），為公司自行揭露數字 ，本行未重新
計算 。Kimberly-Clark則預期 2026年有機銷售成長約落
後 所 屬 品 類 加 權 成 長100bp ， 顯示在品類成長僅約
+1.7% 的環境下價格與組合的操作空間有限 。成長動能
偏向「量」而非「價」；此結構有利於營收穩定，但對毛
利率的支撐較弱。

世界 (5347 TT)

維持評等  
買進(Buy) 
Interposer 貢獻優於預期，VSMC P1 損平點可望提前  
⚫ 3Q26財報、 4Q26財測皆優於預期  
⚫ 產能利用率續增 /漲價 /較佳產品組合有助 減輕 VSMC折舊衝擊  
⚫ AI營收佔比與產品市佔 有望持續提升，維持買進建議  
3Q26財報優於預期：受惠 ASP提升及晶圓出貨優於財測 ，世界 3Q26
營收 157億元 (QoQ +10%、YoY+ 27%)；毛利率則因電源管理占比增至
82%驅動產品組合轉佳 、產能利用率升至 90%、與有利匯率挹注達
33.6 %；營益率 22.1%、EPS  1.6元，財報表現 優於市場預期。 
4Q26營收預估呈高個位數季增 ：受限產能瓶頸，公司預期 4Q26晶圓
出貨量僅季增 0~2 %；ASP則季增 3~5%，高於上季指引，我們認為主
要仍反映成本提升 ；毛利率 34~36%，整體財測優於預期，產能利用
率也預期在終端需求支撐下續增至 90%+。我們另考慮 4Q26匯率區間
可望高於公司預期，估營收達 171億元、毛利率 35.5%、EPS  1.85元。 
AI原動力未變，預期 2027年漲價趨勢延續 ：關於近期 AI展望調整，
管理層認為全球需求並未有任何減少且趨勢並不可逆，只是可能改以
更合理的成長型態發生 。漲價議題上， 除提到不能讓客戶感受不佳，
更希望能讓整個供應鏈都能承擔， 但 2027 年漲價幅度仍有望優於今
年，我們認為主因在於原物料成本、及公司產能利率持續上揚所致。  
VSMC 雖折舊壓力沉重但 Interposer 將有助提前損平 ：VSMC  P1方
面，除重申將自 1Q27量產外，也預期相關折舊可能造成 1H27獲利一
定衝擊，2H27起則隨規模提升逐步減輕。我們認為，雖預期 VSMC P1
將對 1H27毛利率產生高個位數稀釋效果，但預期在 全年 (1)ASP 漲幅
估達雙位數、 (2)產能利用率估近 95%、(3)高毛利率之 Interposer 有望
佔 12吋營收逾 50%，故維持對公司 2027年毛利率近 32%預期。我們
更預期在 VSMC P1 已被 LTA完整覆蓋、及 Interposer 推動此廠損平
點提前來臨下， P2最快也將於 2027年中前宣布展開計畫。 
展望依舊積極且評價已然低檔 ，維持「買進」評等：我們基於
Interposer 對 VSMC P1 帶來之效益優於預期、與更為樂觀的匯率
假設小幅上修公司 2026 /2027 年 EPS 至 6.3 /7.6 元。雖因盤勢 轉
弱改給予較低評價倍數，但 基於中長期展望依舊積極， 故維持「買
進」建議，目標價 調整至 18 0元 (2027 年 EPS* 24倍 PE R)。  
風險因子：漲價程度低於預期 。

強勁客戶需求支持 AI營收佔比 與產品市佔 持續提升： 針對 AI營收佔
比，公司 2025 年約佔低個位數 ，然 2026 年已達雙位數佔比 ，且預估
全年 將略高於 10%；我們更 預期 2027 年在供不應求市況 延續 及
Interposer 貢獻放量 下可望續往 15%邁進。 而在 PMIC 及 Discrete 市
佔上，管理層認為兩者 市佔率都在全球 晶圓廠排名 Top 3 或 Top 5 以
內，其中前者略高於 10%、後者介於 10~15% ，並預期在客戶強勁需
求刺激下 2027 年市佔皆將持續成長。

買進 Buy (B) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力極具吸引力 
增加持股 Overweight (OW) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力略有吸引力 
中立 Neutral (N) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力相似 
降低持股 Underweight (UW) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力稍低 
賣出 Sell (S) 相較於市場指數之回報潛力較低

Document 610: 俄美乌三方会谈在即、特朗普会前密集斡旋，这次会迎来“大和解”吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/俄美乌三方会谈在即、特朗普会前密集斡旋，这次会迎来“大和解”吗？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4

### Page 1

俄美乌三⽅会谈定于 11 ⽉ 10 ⾄ 11 ⽇在阿布扎⽐举⾏。特朗普会前密集出牌：秘密会⻅普京
特使，称将达成涵盖 “ 整个欧洲 ” 的 “ ⼤协议 ” ；特使库什纳赴布鲁塞尔提出五⼤安全⽬标。但
俄⽅专家明确泼冷⽔ —— 此次仅属技术性磋商，领⼟问题 " 绝⽆妥协空间 " 。
摘要：
据新华社报道，俄罗斯总统新闻秘书佩斯科夫 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇确认，俄美乌三⽅围绕乌克兰问题的会谈
将于 11 ⽉ 10 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽇在阿联酋阿布扎⽐举⾏。这是迄今为⽌各⽅就此次会谈时间和地点最为明
确的官⽅表态。
据媒体报道，会谈前⼣，特朗普展开密集外交斡旋。他在⽩宫单独会⻅了俄罗斯总统普京特使
Kirill Dmitriev ，并表⽰，乌克兰战争 “ 接近解决 ” ，预计即将达成的协议将是⼀份 “ ⼤协议 ” （ very
big settlement ），影响范围可能涵盖整个欧洲。与此同时，特朗普特使 Jared Kushner 率团赴布
鲁塞尔，与乌克兰、北约及欧洲伙伴就安全保障和欧洲安全架构举⾏磋商，并公开阐述了美⽅

谈判的五⼤核⼼⽬标。
然⽽，据央视新闻报道，多名俄⽅政治专家在接受采访时明确表⽰，此次阿布扎⽐会谈属于技
术性磋商⽽⾮实质性谈判，在领⼟等核⼼问题上⼏乎没有谈判空间，预计难以取得重⼤突破。
特朗普会前密集出牌，称协议将涉及 " 整个欧洲 "
会谈前⼣，特朗普的外交动作异常密集。
据报道，特朗普在⽩宫与俄罗斯总统普京特使 Kirill Dmitriev 进⾏了私下会⾯，但拒绝透露具体
谈话内容。同⽇，他表⽰： “ 我今天已经打了电话，明天还有电话要打。我们周⼀和周⼆有⼀个
会议。 ”
特朗普再度将可能达成的协议描述为⼀份 “ ⾮常⼤的和解 ” （ very big settlement ），并强调：“ 这将
是⼀份⼤的、美好的协议，所有⼈都会感到满意。它不仅仅涉及乌克兰 …… 我甚⾄可以说，它
可能涉及整个欧洲。 ”
Dmitriev 此前还出席了⽩宫⼀场由第⼀夫⼈梅拉尼娅 · 特朗普组织的活动。该活动旨在纪念梅拉
尼娅参与斡旋的俄控城市阿列什基（ Oleshky ）六天停⽕协议 —— 尽管该协议已于上周⽇到期。

乌克兰议员⼤卫 · 阿拉哈⽶亚（ David Arakhamia ）及⼈权专员德⽶特罗 · 卢⽐内茨（ Dmytro
Lubinets ）也出席了活动。双⽅代表均向梅拉尼娅赠送礼物，并呼吁她继续推动和平努⼒。梅拉
尼娅则向双⽅代表团转交了分别致普京和泽连斯基的信函。梅拉尼娅在活动上表⽰： “ 我们已经
选择了⼈道⽽⾮战争，我们必须⽤带到阿列什基的勇⽓，通过对⽆辜者苦难的共同关切来寻求
共识。 ”
值得注意的是，此次俄美官员的⽩宫会⾯，是美国中期选举后双⽅的⾸次⾯对⾯接触。⺠主党
在此次中期选举中夺回国会控制权，预计将推动加⼤对乌援助并审视特朗普对俄政策。
库什纳布鲁塞尔磋商：五⼤⽬标，尊重主权，北约地位不变
据媒体报道，就在特朗普会⻅ Dmitriev 的同⼀天，特朗普特使库什纳（ Jared Kushner ）率团赴
布鲁塞尔，与乌克兰、北约及欧洲伙伴就安全保障和欧洲安全架构举⾏磋商。库什纳称此次磋
商 “ 富有成效且实质性 ” 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

库什纳随后在社交媒体上发⽂，系统阐述了美⽅谈判的五⼤核⼼⽬标：
在主权与北约问题上，库什纳措辞明确：
“ ⼤和解 ” 路径：库什纳框架被认为现实，但关键在于美⽅是否愿意施压
围绕库什纳公开阐述的五⼤⽬标，分析⼈⼠指出，这⼀框架为俄乌冲突后的欧洲安全架构提供
了相对现实的基础。
据分析，库什纳强调 “ 充分尊重每个国家的独⽴决策权 ” ，意味着美⽅不会明确要求 1997 年前加
⼊北约的成员国将军队从 1997 年后加⼊的成员国撤出，但可能通过其他⽅式对欧洲施加间接压
⼒。
⼀种可能的路径是：美国宣布在近期某⼀时间节点前⼤幅削减对欧能源出⼝，以将燃料重新导
向国内市场，同时告知欧洲恢复俄罗斯管道进⼝ —— 美⽅将在相关管道中持有部分股份，作为
“ 政治保障 ” 。这⼀安排的前提是欧盟释放其冻结的俄罗斯资产。
对于乌克兰，美⽅可能以宣布削减欧盟资助的 " 优先乌克兰需求清单 " （ PURL ）武器出⼝为筹
码，推动乌克兰在语⾔权利和宗教问题上作出让步 —— 这些正是俄⽅所谓 " 去纳粹化 " 议程的⼀部

分。削减对乌武器供应同时也契合特朗普补充美国⾃⾝库存的政策⽬标。
分析认为，上述⼿段符合 “ 美国优先 ” 的政策逻辑，且对美⽅⽽⾔具有 " 合理推诿 " 的空间。但这⼀
路径能否实现，关键在于特朗普是否真正有意愿对欧洲和乌克兰施加实质性压⼒。
普京此前已明确表态：只有当乌克兰将军队从顿涅茨克、卢⽢斯克、扎波罗热和赫尔松地区撤
出，并宣布不加⼊北约，俄⽅才会宣布停⽕并开始谈判。这⼀⽴场与乌克兰及欧洲的底线之
间，仍存在巨⼤鸿沟。
俄⽅专家：这是技术磋商，领⼟问题没有谈判空间
尽管特朗普⽅⾯释放出强烈的乐观信号，俄⽅的预期却明显保守。据央视新闻报道，多名俄⽅
政治专家在接受总台记者采访时，将此次阿布扎⽐会谈定性为 " 技术性磋商 " ，⽽⾮真正意义上的
谈判。
建⽴永久性安全磋商机制（ permanent security consultations ）
建⽴可靠的危机沟通渠道（ reliable crisis communications ）
提升军事透明度（ greater military transparency ）

采取相互降低升级⻛险的措施（ reciprocal measures to reduce escalation risks ）
构建欧洲⻓期安全稳定的持久框架（ an enduring framework for long-term European security
and stability ）
“任何安排都必须充分尊重每个国家的主权、领⼟完整和独⽴决策权。每个国家将保留对⾃⾝安
全和联盟关系的决定权，任何国家都不得对另⼀国的主权选择拥有否决权。北约将继续作为欧洲
集体防御的核⼼，这⼀倡议将在现有机构和承诺的基础上推进。 ”

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
82 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
俄罗斯国际事务委员会主席特列宁表⽰：
据央视新闻报道，俄罗斯总统普京已于 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇在与联邦安全会议成员的会议中，邀请俄⽅谈判
⼩组成员与会，并专⻔讨论了即将举⾏的三⽅会谈。
谈判背景：多轮磋商均未突破核⼼分歧
此次阿布扎⽐会谈并⾮⾸次尝试。据央视新闻梳理，⾃ 2022 年俄乌冲突爆发以来，双⽅已经历
多轮谈判，但核⼼分歧始终未能弥合。
2022 年 3 ⽉，俄乌双⽅在⼟⽿其伊斯坦布尔举⾏第五轮谈判，⼀度在多个关键问题上接近达成⼀
致 —— 俄⽅承诺⼤幅减少在基辅和切尔尼⼽夫⽅向的军事⾏动，乌⽅提出在国际安全保障下确
⽴中⽴地位。然⽽，谈判于当年 4 ⽉中断。
2025 年 3 ⽉，美国分别与俄罗斯、乌克兰在沙特利雅得举⾏穿梭会谈。美⽅声称各⽅在⿊海航⾏

安全及禁⽌攻击能源设施问题上达成⼀致，但三⽅在停⽕条件和安全保障等实质性问题上分歧
依然明显。
此后，俄乌两国代表团于 2025 年 5 ⽉⾄ 7 ⽉间在⼟⽿其伊斯坦布尔进⾏了三轮直接谈判，双⽅未
能在停⽕和⾸脑会⾯等核⼼议题上实现突破，仅同意继续交换战俘及阵亡⼠兵遗体。
2026 年 1 ⽉⾄ 2 ⽉，俄美乌三⽅先后在阿布扎⽐和⽇内⽡举⾏三轮会谈，但因美国和以⾊列对伊
朗发起军事⾏动⽽中断，且除交换战俘外⼏乎未取得实质进展。
2026 年 10 ⽉，谈判出现新动向。美国总统特使 Steve Witkoff 先后在奥斯陆会⻅泽连斯基及欧洲
国家领导⼈，并在圣彼得堡与普京会晤。此后，俄⽅宣布准备于 11 ⽉中期选举后重启三⽅会
谈，乌克兰⽅⾯也表态⽀持尽快启动。
“这不是谈判，⽽是磋商。讨论的是⼀些重要但原则上属于技术性的问题。也就是说，不谈领
⼟，不谈政权性质，也不谈乌克兰内部涉及语⾔及俄罗斯⽂化的规定。 ”

十一月 05, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 金融 ‧ 台灣

元大金 
(2885.TW /2885 TT)

核心獲利成長提升 2026F 配息水準 ; 2027F 維持高 ROE  
重要訊息 
證券、銀行及壽險獲利挹注，帶動元大金 1-3Q26 每股盈餘年增 98%至
3.96 元至歷史新高，1-3Q26 股東權益報酬率年增 8.5 百分點至 19.6%。 
評論及分析  
1-3Q26 獲利年增率 106%，2026F 現金股利上看 3.2-3.3 元。3Q26 金控稅
後淨利季減 17%至 184 億元，但帶動前三季獲利年增率擴大至 106%，其
中證券相關獲利( 證券、投信、期貨)  年增 80%至 155 億元，銀行獲利年增
43%及壽險獲利 3 億元。3Q26 金控每股淨值季增 6.6%至 29.45 元，除獲
利挹注外，主要其他權益季增約 69 億元，優於金控同業。雖 管理層目前
2026F 現金股利配息區間為 50-60%，相較 2025 現金股利配發率達 66%，
凱基預估 2026F 現金股利為 3.2-3.3 元，高於市場預估平均 2.8 元，現金股
利殖利率上看 4.6-4.8%。 
1-3Q26 證券淨收益年增 124%。證券 1-3Q26 經紀手收年增 131%(台股日
均量年增 72%及經紀市占率年增 3.1 百分點 至 16.6%，投資收入年增
118% (套利及方向性之交易策略)，利息收入及其他業務 年增 74%、17%，
獲利成長除台股熱絡外，主要 證券、期貨、投信之 便利交易平台 及較高資本
水準，深化外資法人黏著度並大幅推升 市佔率。另外，金控將 於 12/4 以每
1 股元大投信普通股 換發金控 5.2981 股普通股，將元大投信納為 100%持
股，預估將挹注 2027F 金控獲利約 4%。 
3Q26 銀行核心獲利強勁。3Q26 銀行淨利息及非利息收入各季增 6.9%及
30.4%，其中凱基推估 3Q26F 淨利差季增 2-3 基點至 1.15% -1.16%，反映
外幣聯貸案增加， 2H26F 平均 1.13%以上。由於銀行持續調整放款策略及
手收維持成長動能，公司表示若台灣央行每升息 25 基點提升淨利差 1-2 基
點，加上資產品質穩定，凱基預估 2027F 銀行獲利至少 10%以上成長。  
投資建議  
2026- 27F 證券、期貨及投信持續受惠台股交易熱絡 ，台灣超額儲蓄上看 8.5
兆及 11 兆挹注投信 AUM 及財富管理動能， 銀行淨利息及手收有機會維持
10%以上成長動能。此外，元大金 2026F 現金股利殖利率上看 4.6-4.8%，
2027F 元大金維持 15%以上股東權益報酬率。綜合以上看法，調升元大金
至「增加持股」評等，目標價 82 元，基於 2026F-27F 股價淨值比 2.4X。 
投資風險  
台股成交量及投資收益低於預期；銀行信貸成本高於預期 。 
增加持股‧ 調升

圖 31: 公司概況  圖 32: 組織結構圖 
元大金控為證券為主體之金控公司，前身為復華金控，元大集團
於 2007 年 4 月完成合併復華金控，目前旗下涵蓋的業務範圍橫
跨證券、銀行、期貨、壽險。元大過去積極併購擴大業務及資產
規模，2005 併購復華金控，2011 併購寶來證券，台灣經紀市占
率一舉突破 14%，2013 陸續併購台灣國際紐約人壽跨入壽險版
圖，2014 證券業務拓展至韓國，併購韓國東洋證券， 2016/3 併
購大眾銀行，擴大銀行業務。 2025 年 12 月資產達 4.29 兆台
幣，台灣 14 家金控中排名第 11，為台灣最大之證券為主體金控
公司。

### Page 11

台灣 元大金

十一月 05, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 11

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
2020 年 COVID-19 爆發後，元大金
年初開始執行各項防疫應變措
施，使 2020 年能源耗用相較 2019 
年增加 16.45%、能源密集度增加 
8.38%。 
購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
本年度元大金合計購買 51 萬度的
再生能源，相當於減少 259.59 公
噸 CO2。 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3) 
2020年溫室氣體排放量為 3,880.29 
公噸 CO2e。 2020年初因 COVID-19 
疫情爆發元大金啟動異地辦公，
在人員調動頻繁及作業型態異動
下，致使 2020 年碳放排量之絕對
值較 2019 年增加。 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
2020年正職員工離職率 10.96%，
近三年(2017-2020年)呈現逐年下
降趨勢 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性主管比例為 53.2%，於初階管
理階層之佔比為 60.6%，於高階管
理階層之佔比為 35% 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
2020年集團員工總人數為 10,944 
人，其中男性員工比例 35.5%，女
性員工比例 64.5% 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

### Page 1

十一月 06, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  IC設計 ‧ 台灣

聯詠 
(3034.TW /3034 TT)

看不到隧道盡頭的光  
重要訊息 
聯詠召開 3Q26法說會。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 財報符合預期。3Q26 營收 310 億元，季增 8.1%、年增 26.0%，主因
智慧型手機季節性備貨，帶動 SMDDIC 出貨增加。毛利率為 40.9%，季減
1.5 個百分點、年增 4.6 個百分點，季減主要受 SoC 比重下降，以及成本上
升與 ASP 調整之間存在時間落差所影響。加計營業費用 55.8 億元，使 3Q26 
EPS 達 9.99 元，季減 3.9%、年增 66.3%，符合凱基預估。 
4Q26 毛利率面臨更多逆風 。管理階層預期 4Q26 營收達 280-292 億元區
間、毛利率達 37-39%，以中位數計算分別季減 7.7%與 2.9 個百分點，三
大產品線營收均呈季減，主因 ：(1)平板庫存調整，以及非中國手機客戶庫存
去化，部分被中國手機客戶 OLED DDIC 需求增加所抵銷，使 SMDDI 產品線
相對具韌性；( 2)大尺寸面板 SoC 出貨季減，非面板 SoC 出貨持平；及 (3)大
尺寸顯示器需求季減，尤其 筆電受 1H26 提前拉貨及漲價壓抑終端需求，使
LDDIC 產品線表現最為疲弱。我們僅微幅上修 4Q26 營收預估至公司財測低
緣，反映價格調整，但我們對消費性電子需求的看法仍 較為保守。我們 下修
4Q26 毛利率預估至 38.6%，季減 2.3 個百分點，主因：( 1)低成本庫存已消
耗完畢；( 2)大尺寸面板 SoC 出貨量下滑；及(3) KGD 成本大幅上升，稀釋
SoC 毛利率。  
我們仍保守看待 2027 年的需求。儘管有新產品支撐，包括車用 eDP TDDI 、
TED、折疊手機 OLED TDDI 、平板及筆電的整合式 解決方案，以及獨立式顯
示 IC 和 tTED 類產品，我們對 消費性 IC 設計產業仍保守看待。我們認為新
產品貢獻難以完全抵銷多重逆風，包括：( 1) AI 帶動的通膨壓抑消費性電子
需求；( 2)記憶體價格 維持高檔，推升終端產品售價，恐衝擊智慧型手機、
PC 及筆電出貨；( 3)晶圓代工與封測漲價持續，且公司表示供需情況仍具挑
戰；及(4)美國期中選舉結果可能大幅改變市場展望。因此，我們調整 2027
年營收預估至 1,103 億元，年減 1.2%，毛利率下修至 39.1%，年減 1.2 個
百分點。  
投資建議 
考量： (1)保守看待終端需求； 及(2)新品貢獻難以抵銷多項營運逆風， 我們
下修 2027 年 EPS 預估至 30.71 元(年減 10.6%)。目標價為 460 元，基於
2027 年 EPS 預估平均之 15 倍不變換算得出。維持「持有」評等。  
投資風險  
總經不確定性； OLED DDI 市場競爭減少。  
持有‧維持 
  收盤價 November 6 (NT$)   495.5

前次目標價  (NT$) 470.0

7.2

焦點內容   
1. 3Q26 財報符合預期。 
2. 4Q26 毛利率面臨更多逆風。 
3. 我們仍保守看待 2027年的需求。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：產品組合 
聯詠成立於 1997 年，於 2002 年上市。公司已成為全球平面
顯示器驅動 IC (DDI) 與系統單晶片 (SoC)解決方案的龍頭供應
廠。受惠於台灣的先進矽晶圓代工服務，聯詠已成功擴大產品
線並獲得國際平面顯示器大廠的重點設計訂單。為了滿足從行
動裝置到大型家用設備的消費電子需求，公司提供全系列產
品，公司表示 2020 年在中國大尺寸面板廠的市占 率為
24.1%。

ESG圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 = 8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，公司資料

### Page 1

十一月 06, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

法說會重點摘要

華新 
(1605 TT, NT$31.45, 未評等)

重點摘要  
1. 3Q26 每股盈餘 1.78 元，大幅年增、但季減；其中， 電子業轉投資
收益貢獻每股盈餘達 1.79 元，抵銷本業虧損。  
2. 部門別展望： (1) 不鏽鋼： 預計台灣和 歐洲 4Q26 營運季增，中國
季持平； (2) 資源：鎳產品售價持續低檔震盪，但 部分廠商已開始
自律減產， 將有助鎳生鐵重返供需平衡；及  (3) 電線電纜： 4Q26
營運將延續穩健態勢 。 
3. 華新世紀核能  (公司持股佔比過半 )，初期將整合所有股東材料提供
能力，著重於零組件供應； 未來目標成為串聯台灣核能供應鏈重要
平台，打造台灣低碳能源產業生態，並與國際接軌 。 
結論 
4Q26 不鏽鋼旺季營運回升，但資源事業恐成為拖累變因。整體而言，
公司獲利仍仰賴電子業轉投資收益與處分貢獻 。

3Q26 財報 
 3Q26 每股盈餘 1.78 元，大幅年增、 但季減；其中，電子業轉投資收益
貢獻每股盈餘 達 1.79 元，抵銷本業虧損。營收年 增 12%、但淡季季減
7%至 462 億元。營利率 -1.5% ，年增 0.4、但季減 2.4 個百分點 。 
 部門別營收比重 ：不鏽鋼 49% 、電線電纜 31% 、資源 18%、其他
2%。 
 地區別營收佔比：台灣 40-50%、歐洲 25-30%，其餘來自中國。  
 部門別營收成長 率：(1) 不鏽鋼： 年增 、但 季減 ，主要受歐洲暑 休影
響；(2) 線纜： 年增、季持平，受惠 AI 建廠商機； (3) 資源：年增、 但
季減，反映售價持平，但冰鎳出貨量下滑 ；及 (4) 銅線材：年增、季
增。 
 部門別毛利率： (1) 不鏽鋼： 年增、但季減至 5%，主因歐洲暑休使銷
量下滑； (2) 資源：年 減、季減至 0%，主因 為鎳礦成本 上漲，對比 鎳
生鐵售價高檔震盪，且冰鎳銷量減少；及  (3) 電線線纜：大致 年持平、
季持平為 11%。 
 部門別獲利： (1) 不鏽鋼：虧損擴大； (2) 資源：轉虧損； (3) 電線電
纜：年 持平、季持平；及  (4) 其他： 電子業轉投資收益貢獻 79 億元。 
 總資產季 增 37 億元；其中， 現金及應收帳款分別 減少 13 億、3 億元，
存貨增加 17 億元 (係因預付購料 款增加)，長期投資增加 32 億元 (主要
為權益法 投資收益 79 億元， 抵銷上市櫃 有價證券市價 減少 84 億元， 以
及轉投資華邦 ECB 持有人轉換成普通股產生權益增加約 32 億元) 。 
 總負債季增 7 億元，主因銀行借款增加 42 億元，而各項應付帳款減少
12 億元。

重要免責聲明，詳見最終頁

### Page 2

台灣 華新

十一月 06, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

 3Q26 收現及銷貨日數皆季 增，但流動比率及長期資金適足率保持在合
理區間，整體財務結構穩健。 
 3Q26 資本支出 30 億元，多數用於高雄海纜廠及不鏽鋼設備投資。

營運展望更新  
 不鏽鋼事業：(1) 台灣：隨庫存去化持續及需求逐漸回升，預計 4Q26
營運 季增 ；(2) 中國： 儘管內 需不足及 工業 市場復甦緩慢 ，但冷精棒品
牌經營發展效益逐漸顯現， 預計 4Q26 表現季 持平； 及 (3) 歐洲：暑休
結束後需求 將逐步回溫，預計 4Q26 營運季增 。 
 歐洲碳關 稅 (CBAM) 和防衛保護措施對中國製程最不利，因其 碳排量最
高。歐洲或台灣 採用廢鋼冶煉 的廠商 ，碳邊境關稅較低。  
 預計 2027 年冷精棒銷售年增 20-30%，有助縮小中國不鏽鋼虧損。  
 資源事業：鎳產品售價持續低檔震盪，部分鎳冶煉廠商已因陷入負現金
利潤而自律 減產；加上一些不具 自有電廠的鎳廠商 3Q-4Q26 也陸續減
產 20-30% ，有利鎳生鐵重返供需平衡。華 新自帶電廠，會計利潤損
平；若未來鎳價回升，獲利將隨之改善。  
 印尼鎳礦配額，今年初為 2.6 億噸，年底放寬至 2.9-3.0 億噸。若明年
鎳礦配額不足，公司菲律賓廠可作為支援。  
 電線電纜事業： 預計 4Q26 營運延續 穩健態勢 ，反映高科技產業 建廠需
求及強韌電網建設支撐相關產品出貨維持高檔。 目前高壓線纜產能 達滿
載，楊梅新廠產能 (中低壓 線纜) 已爬坡 70-80%。 
 AI 需求爆發帶來電力與算立基礎建設需求快速增加，推升電力傳輸、
HVDC 高壓電纜和 AI 液冷系統訂單，有利公司不鏽鋼及電纜事業增添
新營運動能，且轉投資電子元件業務獲利穩健成長。  
 為因應台電強韌電網 2029-30 年需求高峰，公司有一條高壓線纜產線正
在裝機中。  
 民間科技建廠需求亦在增加中，一般線纜廠規模不足，華新為少數具 供
貨能力之廠商 。 
 離岸風電方面， 每 1.0-1.5GW 電力需海底電纜長度 300 公里。海纜業
務預計 2027 年 6-9 月取得認證並進入 量產，出貨 因配合工程進度與海
象，可能 落在 2028 年。 
 公司銅線材在接單時即鎖定銅價與毛利率，因此銅價波動影響有限。  
 華新世紀核能  (持股佔比過半 )，首先將整合所有股東材料提供能力；其
中，華新英國子公司 SMP 可提供核能用鋼材，德國和 中國廠目前也皆
有供應核電廠使用的無縫管。 該公司 初期將著重於 零組件供應， 未來目
標成為串聯台灣核能供應鏈重要平台 ，打造 台灣低碳能源產業生態，並
與國際接軌。  
 華新世紀核能另二大 股東，世紀能源優勢為 擁有 5-600 位高階焊工，且
臨海靠港腹地大，未來可進行重大設備組裝與出口；高力強項則在於
SOFC 板式熱換器與高溫容器爐銲，這些核心能力皆 可在核能相關設備
上發揮所長。

法說會重點摘要

合勤控 
(3704 TT, NT$37.25, 未評等)

重點摘要  
1. 合勤控 3Q26 合併營收 77.53 億元，QoQ-11.4% 、YoY+20.8% ，
季減主因運營商面對記憶體大幅漲價、重新評估資本支出而自
3Q26 起放緩拉貨；毛利率 25.5% ，QoQ+1.8ppts 、YoY-4.3ppts ，
季增來自產品組合，年減則受記憶體等材料漲價影響；稅後 淨利
4.69 億元，QoQ-8.6% 、YoY-15.5% ，EPS 1.15 元。 
2. 公司表示 4Q26 寬頻產品扣除少數大型運營商拉貨放緩後， Tier 2/3
客戶需求仍在增加，仍有季增機會，國防標案亦將有營收入帳。  
3. 2027 年成長動能來自深化一級電信商合作、拓展南亞 /南美/東南亞
/紐澳市場及國防業務；產品端已進入大型電信商 Wi-Fi 8 專案名
單，預計 2027 年出貨；越南製造基地已進入試產，2027 年正式加
入產能。 
結論 
我們認為合勤控 3Q26 營收季減主因運營商因記憶體漲價放緩拉貨，而
非終端需求真正轉弱，後續驗證點為 4Q26 寬頻拉貨回溫幅度、記憶體
成本轉嫁能力；中長期則觀察 Wi-Fi 8 與 50G PON 專案 2027 年出貨進
度、越南產能開出，以及國防業務的成長延續性。

3Q26 營運成果  
 3Q26 合併營收 77.53 億元，QoQ-11.4% 、YoY+20.8% ，季減主因
客戶產品需求放緩，年增則來自今年客戶對產品需求增加 
 毛利率 25.5% ， QoQ+1.8ppts ，主要為產品組合貢獻， YoY-
4.3ppts ，主因記憶體等材料成本漲價及產品銷售組合影響 
 營業費用 14.06 億元，較 3Q25 的 14.34 億元略減、較 2Q26 的
13.67 億元增加， 公司表示目前每季費用規模約 14 億元，費用管控
為維持獲利能力與長期競爭力的核心政策之一  
 營業利益 5.72 億元， QoQ-19.1% 、 YoY+18.7% ，營業利益率
7.4% 
 稅後淨利 4.69 億元，QoQ-8.6% 、YoY-15.5% ，EPS 1.15 元

產品與區域營收組合  
 3Q26 產品營收比重為寬頻產品 74%、企業網通產品 14%、其他產
品 12% 
 寬頻設備營收約 57.15 億元，YoY +15%、QoQ-20% 
 企業網通營收約 11.08 億元，YoY +26%，主要受客戶需求持續
升溫

 其他產品( 以國防為主) 營收約 9.3 億元，QoQ +83%，主要來自客
戶需求回溫  
 3Q26 區域營收比重為歐洲 47% 、美洲 14% 、新興市場與亞太
39% 
 歐洲營收 36.47 億元，YoY +3%、QoQ+8% 
 美洲營收 10.74 億元，YoY +5%、QoQ-2% 
 新興市場與亞太營收 30.32 億元，YoY +65%，為年增主要來源

電信營運商產品與技術布局  
 公司已進入大型電信商 Wi-Fi 8 專案名單，正配合客戶測試，預期
2027 年出貨 
 公司整合局端光纖 OLT 、雲端軟體及對應終端產品，已完成 50G 
PON 混合模式產品開發，並已有歐洲客戶指定採用、合作開發  
 歐洲正進行光纖替換，公司產品組合完整，可承接升級需求  
 相同技術亦可應用於資料中心管理介面，公司正開拓該市場  
 無人機通訊模組已開始小量出貨

企業網通與訂閱服務  
 公司觀察 2026-2027 年 SMB 企業網路將出現升級需求： Wi-Fi 7 於
2026 年已在企業網路大量普及，公司將於 2027 年發表企業級 Wi-
Fi 8 產品 
 Wi-Fi 8 可望帶動 2.5G ／5G／10G 高速及高功率 PoE 接取交換機
需求 
 企業網路更新亦將帶動下一世代 UTM 資安防火牆換機 
 公司自評為少數可提供資安防火牆、有線交換機到無線網路整體方
案的台灣業者  
 訂閱制軟體授權服務每年皆有高雙位數成長，為公司持續發展的目
標

2027 年展望  
 成長面：深化一級電信商合作；持續拓展南亞、南美、東南亞及紐
澳市場；持續深耕國防市場 
 營運韌性：越南製造基地已於 2026 年啟動、目前試產， 2027 年正
式加入產能，可提升產能配置彈性、降低地緣政治風險並強化歐美
市場競爭力；同時強化供應商與物料管控  
 獲利面：以優化產品組合與控制成本結構確保利潤率； Wi-Fi 8 與
50G PON 新世代產品正與全球客戶測試，雲端管理與資安服務則拓
展訂閱收入

### Page 3

台灣 合勤控

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

QA 
 3Q26 營收季減的原因？客戶放緩拉貨是否延續至 4Q26 ？ 
 2Q26 營收顯著成長，但記憶體成本漲幅大於客戶與製造商原先
預期，公司須與客戶協商漲價，運營商因而重新考慮資本支出與
市場布建，自 3Q26 起放緩拉貨 
 4Q26 寬頻產品扣除幾家大 型運營商拉貨放緩，Tier 2 /3 客戶需求
仍在增加，仍有季增機會  
 國防標案是否會有營收入帳？ 
 會有營收入帳  
 2027 年寬頻與企業網通何者成長較高？  
 兩條產品線 2027 年均以持續成長規劃，公司未區分成長高低  
 南亞客戶標案能否更上一層樓？還有哪些區域帶動成長？ 
 南亞不會只針對單一客戶或單一產品，將開發更多客戶與產品線
以提升市占與營收  
 2027 年南美、東南亞及紐澳為主要成長動能  
 半導體零組件備料期間多長？ 
 非常長；受半導體產能排擠，主動、被動元件與記憶體交期都很
長，公司須提高安全庫存，使存貨金額相對提升  
 3Q26 是否以高成本記憶體生產？  
 存貨提升屬計畫性策略備料，用以控制記憶體成本、提升標案競
爭力 
 各大運營商對記憶體持續漲價已超出可承受範圍，公司將視客戶
對成本的反應調整  
 主要生產基地？  
 目前主要生產基地在中國 無錫 
 其他類產品的組成？是否全為國防業務？  
 公司表示營運成長是全面性的，並非單一來自國防  
 國防占其他類大部分但非全部；國防絕大部分為內銷，最大客戶
為中科院 
 國防技術涵蓋通訊( 保密通訊) 、雷達 (搜索與追蹤) 及電子戰( 干擾
與反干擾) 三類，公司已投入約 20 年，從樣品測試到量產常需十
幾年 
 國防產品外銷狀況？  
 歐洲尚未外銷 ，目前僅小部分外銷中東、尚未放量，成長主要來
自國內

### Page 1

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

法說會重點摘要

環宇-KY 
(4991 TT, NT$ 367.5,  未評等)

重點摘要  
1. 3Q26 營收 US$26.4mn （新台幣 8.45 億元），QoQ +8.4% 、YoY 
+46.5% ；InP 基板與磊晶供應回穩， AOC 營收 QoQ +25.8% ，營
收比重由 2Q26 的 61%回升至 71%，帶動毛利率由 43.3% 回升至
47.0% ，營業利益率 19.3% ， EPS 1.33 元。產能利用 率超過
100% 
2. 4Q26 財測營收 US$30 -34mn 、毛利率 46-50% 、營業費用
US$7.6- 8.6mn ，動能來自 AOC 需求持續強勁及威力富新增產能。
200G PD 驗證已全數完成，已有客戶開始上量，導入第二家基板來
源後預期 2Q27 起有顯著營收貢獻；100mW CW Laser 依客戶回饋
調整，預計 3Q27 小批量、4Q27 大批量出貨 
3. 公司 2027 年 InP 基板已全數鎖定，變數在出口核准流程，非中國
基板來源預計 2028 年後才達有意義比例；威力富增資擴產與美國
廠無塵室擴充預計 2Q27 完成。 
結論 
我們認為 3Q26 基板供應回穩已使 AOC 出貨與毛利率重回正軌，
4Q26 財測中位數 US$32mn 隱含 QoQ 約 21% 成長，在稼動率超過
100% 、2027 年基板已鎖定下，短期營收動能明確。 2027 年的關鍵驗
證點在於 200G PD 於 2Q27 放量、100mW CW Laser 於 2H27 出貨，
以及擴產於 2Q27 如期完成；非中國基板供應仍需時間、出口核准可
能遞延，費用亦將隨 CW Laser 攤銷與 RSA 酬勞成本墊高。整體而
言，我們看好公司在 AI 資料中心光通訊需求擴張下，憑藉 InP PD 規格
優勢與產能布局延續穩定成長

經營環境與供應鏈策略  
 公司以美國為主要生產基地，認為未來若有政治面限制，對公司屬
正面 
 2Q26 的 InP 基板供應問題目前已趨穩定，但公司不認為未來必然
維持穩定，持續開拓不同基板來源、vendor 與生產基地，確保供應
再度受限時仍能應變並持續供貨 
 產能稼動率超過 100% ，供不應求； 11/3 董事會通過對威力富增
資，以增加產能並補齊設備，定位為美國以外的重要生產基地；美
國廠亦將擴充 cleanroom 並補齊瓶頸機台，預計 2Q27 完成

3Q26 財務表現  
 3Q26 營收新台幣 8.45 億元，QoQ 以美元計+8.37% 、以新台幣計
+9.7% ；YoY 以美元計+46.5% 、以新台幣計+55.4%

 毛利率 47.0% ，QoQ +3.7ppts ，主因： 
 AOC 銷量增加，營收比重由 2Q26 的 61%升至 71% 
 AOC 反映原料成本上升而調漲售價  
 認列的存貨跌價及呆滯損失減少 
 營業費用 2.35 億元，QoQ -0.2% 、YoY +53% ，主因 RSA 依會計
準則認列較高酬勞成本，以及員工與董事酬勞估列增加；營業費用
率 27.8% ，研發費用率由 2Q26 的 10.1% 降至 6.9% 
 營業利益 1.63 億元，營業利益率 19.3% ；稅後淨利 1.55 億元，歸
屬母公司淨利 1.59 億元，EPS 1.33 元（2Q26 0.92 元、3Q25 0.13
元） 
 業外收入 3,120 萬元，QoQ -12.2% ，主因對上海宙鎵光電認列的
投資利益減少；YoY 由虧轉盈，因自 1Q26 起公司對另一家轉投資
公司不再具重大影響力、改列 FVOCI ，認列的投資損失減少  
 1-9M26 營收 23.29 億元，YoY 以美元計+52.1% ；毛利率
48.1% ，YoY +3.7ppts ；稅後淨利 4.56 億元，EPS 3.91 元（2025
年同期- 0.45 元 ）；ROE 15.63%  
 9/30/26 現金及約當現金 25.10 億元，YoY +144% ，來自營業現金
流入與 1Q26 現金增資；3Q26 營業現金流 1.03 億元，低於 2Q26
的 2.61 億元，主因應收帳款增加尚未收現，應收帳款周轉天數 45
天，仍在合理授信期間內

產品別營收變化  
 AOC ：QoQ +25.8% ，2Q26 受基板供應限制導致磊晶原料短缺，
3Q26 基板與磊晶供應回穩後銷量回升；YoY +39% ，受惠 data 
center 與 AI 需求增加 
 RF Foundry ：QoQ -7.64% ，因 1Q26 部分出貨遞延至 2Q26 墊高
基期；YoY +98% ，來自平均代工價格提高與出貨增加 
 Opto Foundry ：QoQ -37%，公司調整接單客戶，改以重要客戶或
未來預計投片生產的客戶為主；YoY -2.2% 
 3Q26 營收比重 RF Foundry 20% 、 Opto Foundry 6% 、 AOC 
71%、其他 3%

4Q26 財測 
 營收 US$30 -34mn ，動能來自 AOC 需求持續強勁，以及投資威力
富帶來的額外產能  
 毛利率 46-50%，主力產品仍為 AOC 
 營業費用 US$7.6- 8.6mn ，高於 3Q26 的 US$7.3mn ，因 100mW 
CW Laser 遞延，相關研發費用自 4Q26 起開始攤銷

### Page 3

台灣 環宇-KY

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

200G PD 與 CW Laser 進度 
 200G PD ： 
 所有驗證已完成，已有 1 家客戶開始上量  
 原基板廠商自身產能提升不如預期（與出口管制無關），將增加
另一家廠商的同規格基板作為第二來源，並進行 incremental  
verification  
 預期 2Q27 起大批量出貨並有顯著營收貢獻  
 100mW CW Laser 依客戶回饋調整，未來半年聚焦可靠度驗證與客
戶送樣，預計 3Q27 小批量、 4Q27 大批量出貨； 70mW 仍在
sample 階段，廠內主力為 100mW ，70mW 由合作夥伴供應 
 100G PD 自 9M26 出貨回升， 4Q26 多個生產基地同時供貨，公司
對穩定供貨具信心；2026 年 100G PD 市占與 2025 年相近，2027
年雖可能因客戶調整供應鏈略受影響，但公司有信心至少維持市占

InP 基板供應與技術定位  
 2027 年基板已全數鎖定，基板並非公司直接採購，而是由磊晶廠
商以長約向基板廠商鎖量，並涉及 prepayment 條件；變數在政治
面，出口需逐筆核准，流程可能遞延，但 2027 年整體供應量無虞  
 2027 年中國以外的基板供應仍未達合理水準，仍需仰賴中國出
口，2028 年後可望明顯改善；限制出貨的是基板，磊晶、晶圓製
造與後段均非瓶頸，基板廠產能亦持續倍增，問題在政治面  
 基板價格持續上漲，材料成本會反映給客戶，惟調價速度有時跟不
上材料漲幅  
 競爭優勢在良率與效能，規格優於同業，可協助客戶在模組設計上
節省 component 成本；公司以 4 吋晶圓生產，同業多為 2-3 吋，
價格具競爭力  
 InP PD 不會被完全取代：SiGe PD 約可做到 200G ，模組設計較複
雜且受傳輸距離限制，400G PD 仍將以 InP 為主；客戶選擇公司首
重品質、交期與產能，價格為次要

QA 
 公司對市場與業務的展望？ 
 2026- 2027 年預期為相當穩定的成長  
 客戶端幾個主要 player 短期內不會有太大變化  
 即使美國祭出 FCC 限制，也不會隔天即實施，會有過渡期，各
方都預期此事會發生並已做適當布局，整體展望沒有太大變化  
 100mW CW Laser 的驗證與出貨進度？為何遞延？  
 100mW 進度有所遞延，原因是客戶回饋需要做調整

 未來半年多聚焦可靠度驗證，以及對客戶送樣與客戶端

有問題

 稼動率超過 100% ，供不應求

去差異不大，仍在合理的客戶授信期間內

 營運主體在美國子公司，收付款以美元為主，新台幣波動影響不
大 
 公司產能擴充規劃？

 美國廠將擴充 cleanroom 並補齊瓶頸機台，預計 2Q27 完成
